PREFACE TO FIRST EDITION
Prasna Sastra or Horary Astrology is not only of popular
appeal but of basic importance because it can answer questions of immediate consequence affecting our everyday life.
The English translation of Prasna Tantra, herewith presented
with copious notes and illustrations, is intended to fulfil a
long-felt want not only by professional astrologers but also
by the astrological students.
The mechanism of interpretation given in Presne tantra
1 1
has been tested by me in a large number of cases and found
quite satisfactory.
Of late the Market is being increasingly flooded with ail
sorts of cheap astrological literature lacking genuine scholarship, the main object being, some sorts of sensationmongering. Fottunatelv the number of educated people
inclined ta give serious attention ta the scientific aspects of
astrology is also increasing. And this book is mainly intended
ta meet bath scholarly demanda and the practical needs of
astrological students, amateur and professional.
The astrologer who uses horary astrology should be able
to counsel his consulters on the basis of his findings scientifically made, and it is hoped that this book will be received
by the educated public with the same warmth with which ail
my other writings have baen received ail thèse years.
8-8-1969 B. V. RAMAN
PREFACE TO SIXTH EDITION
1 am happy to present herewith the Sixth Edition of
the English translation of PRASNA TANTRA which has been
out of print for some time. The fact that a new edition has
been ca lied for within such a short period bears testimony
ta the fact there is increasinq : interest evinced by the
educated public for the study and appreciation of astroloqv
as a useful branch of knowledge.
PRASNA TANTRA is a unique publication of great utility
especia)ly in the matter of dealing with problems arising in
our daily lives.
tt is hoped thar the educated public will extend ta
PRAS NA TANTRA the same cordial reception that they have
do ne to ail my other writings.
BANGALORE -8. V. RAMAN
GENERAL INTRODUCTION
Horary astrology is the mos] important branch of the
three divisions' of the astroloqical science, the other two
being Jetek« (predictive astrology) and Muhurtha (electional
astroloav). It takes as the basis for predicting future events,
the horoscope set for the moment a query is put. Horaev has
a further advantaqe over the judicial in the sense that the
time of query is known accuratelv. ln fact the time at which
a .querv is put is the time of birth of the intention or desire
in the mind of the querent and hence of great significance.
. The question must be seriously put if the answer is earnestly
desired and if correct results are to be obtained. Any
questions posed light-heartedly or with mischievous intention
should be disrnissed by the astrologer. Horary astrology is
the art of perceiving the relation between the thought as it
arises in the mind and the pattern of the heavens at the
moment. This gives a clue for forecasting an event. Hence
horaryastrology is the most practical and useful branch of
knowledge.
, Prasna Tantra, an English translation of which is herewith
presented, is a treatise on horary astrology, written by
Neelakanta Daivagnya. According to some authorities, it is
not an independent treatise. but a part of Neelakanta's larger
work -1fiaka Neelakanteeya, being the 3rd or last Tantra or
division, the other two being Samgnatantra (preliminarias)
ix
,y
1.
1
and Varshatantra (the annuel horoscope). Whe!her an independent treatise or a part of a larqer work, it has its own
uniqueness as dealing clearly and in simple language with
important aspects of horary astrology.
There are any number of treatises on this branch of
astrology written by ancient rnasters. such as Chappanna or
Shatpanchasika, Lampaka, Prasnagnana, Prasnabhushana,
Prasnasindhu, Presns Chintamani,. Bhuvana Deepik«,
Jinendramala. Krishneeya and Prasna Marga, the last being
the most comprehensive and elaborate exposition of Horary.
Prasna Marga has been translate by me into English with
elaborate notes and it is expected to be out by the end of
1979.
My choice of Fresne Tantra for translation into English
was due to three reasons. First, it is compact, yet comprehensive. Second, the treatise is clear and the principles given
in its pages are in a large measure applicable' to modern
conditions. And third, in my practice most of the predictions
made by me essentially based on this book have been
remarkably fulfilled.
The author of this book is said to be Neelakanta
Daivagnya. From his own Tajaka and from other collateral
sources, it has beén gathered that he came from a family of
astrologers. He was the grandson of Chinthamani Dalvaqnya,
son of Anantha Daivagnya and brother of Rama Daiva~hya
and belonged to the Gothra of Gargya. ln the last part of
his Varshatantra, Neelakanta records that he composed this
book on the eighth day of the bright ha If of Aswija of Saka
Year 1509 which means 1567 A.D. There is also evidence
that he hailed from Vidarbha and that he was about 43 or 44
years old WhElO he wrote this book. ' While the first two parts
x
(of\ his larger work). viz., Samgnatantra and Varshatantra have
the.cornmentarles of one Viswanathà Daivagnya, probably
written in Saka 1551 (A.D. 1639), no commenta ries have
been written . on the Iast ' division which may be sa id to
constitute the independent volume of Prasna Tantra. ln
translating the original into English, the main object has been
to bring the spirit of the author rather than the .letter.
Astrology . is a technical subject and in attempting to
convey ideas from a highly suggestive and perfect lanquaqe
like Sanskrit into a modern and developing language like
English certain confusion of sense cannot be entirely ruled
. out. It is not aliterai translation 1 have given. But it is a
liberal rendering, the object being to make accessible to the
Indian public who have a limited knowledge in Sanskrit and
also to the Western world where, in recent times, interest in
the study of Hindu astrology has been growing by leaps and
bounds, a system of Horary astrology in which they will find
sufficient information of practical utilitv, It is for the learned
readers to judge how far 1 have been successful in my humble
endeavours.
r=«. Tantra has been divided into four chapters, viz.,
Prasna Vichara (preliminaries), Bhava Prasna (questions
bea,ing on different houses), Visesha Prasna (special
questions) and Prakirnakadhyaya (concluding remarks).
The first chapter deals with such details as the utili'ty of
Prasna, planetary characteristics and avasthas, planetary
natures, 'and matters to be inquired into from different
houses,
The second chapter is quite comprehensive. It gives
combinations for judging the outcome of 'questions bearing
on different houses.
xi
The third chanter deals with specific questions such as
the return of a man gone aVllay from home; whether he is
. alive or dead ; whether or not he will return ; illness; whether
the patient will recover, .nature of illness; diagnosis, eté.;
leaving ernplovrnent and seeking another job; disputes, litigation, etc. ; success and failure; theft and loss of articles, the
age, sex, etc., of the thiet.. whether the article is lost completely or recoverable and if latter how it could be recovered;
children ; marrîaqe : dreams, food. hunting, quarrels, incarceration, about ships on sea, purchase and sale, planting of
crops, etc. The fourth chapter covers questions such as :
gain of money, general· outlook, time of gain, thought-/
reading, enquiry about women, nature of intimacy with,.
women, nature of weather and crops, etc. The chapter is
concluded with some important information on aspects and
yogas considered in Tajaka Astrology.
The treatment throughout has been comprehensive. Sornetimes there is also a certainjurnblinq of subjects dealt with.
For instance, while in Chapter Il questions bearing on
marriage and children are treated, the sa me subjects are
again repeated in Chapter III in greater detail. On the whole,
the entire gamut of horary astrology has been covered skilfully leaving to the discretion of the astroloqer, how best he
could adapt the principles to answer questions even not
covered in the book.
The aspects considered in this book are those of the
Tajaka system. The principle aspects, to put in simple
language, are' the sextile (60°), square (90°), opposition
(180°), trine (120°) and conjunction (0°). An aspect by itself
has no orb. But, planets have orbs of operation (deeptharnsas}.
They are: the Sun 15°, the Moon 12°, Mars 7°, Mercury 7°,
xii
Jupiter go, Venus 7° and Saturn 80• No mundane aspects are
considered. Though the nature of an aspect and conjunction
may be said to depend upon the nature of the planets in
aspect, the square and opposition are generally held to be
inauspieious while the trine and sextile are held to give rise
to auspicious results. Conjunctions are favourable with benefie pla nets and evil with malefic planets. For instance, a conjunetion of Saturn and Mars is definitely evil and threatens
serious consequences. lA conjunction of Jupiter and Venus is
equally good and denotes auspiciousness.
ln horary astrology, planets become distinguished as
significators. For instance, the lord of the 3rd is the siqnlficator of brothers. If t'le happens to be, say Saturn, and the
ascendant lord is Jupiter and if théy are in conjunction, the
brother (signified by Jupiter) would be adversely aftected if
the question relates to mutual relations of the two brothers.
Yogas in Tajaka system arise on the basis of applying
exact ànd separating aspects. For further details 1 would
. refer my readers to my book Varshaphal or The Hindu
Progressed Horoscope. It deals fully with the Tajaka system.
As reqards the significations of planets, Neelakanta has
no doubt dealt with them in a fairly exhaustive manner. But
we can also have recourse to the significations suggested in
. other authoritative c1assics such as Prasna Marga and
Krishneeya.
1 n interpretation of horarvcharts certain niceties have ro
be observed. .The lord of the ascendant is always the significator of the querant or his agent though the ascendant and
the planets in the ascendant are also important as eo-significators. The significator of the event is the ruler of the house
concerned. For example the signifieator of marriage is the
xiii
lord of the 7th though planets occupvinq the 7th will also
have a role of their own to play. Matters pertaining to health
can be studied from the 1 st house but illness is indicated by
the 6th house. Money matters come under the 2nd but lands
and immovable property are signified by the 4th house.
Further details about events signified by different houses can . be found in the body of the text.
If a querent puts a question, say pertaining to his
brother's wife, then we have to éonsult the 9th hou se as it is
the 7th from the 3rd house. Similarlya stolen article belonging to a friend has reference to the 4th from 11 th (friend),
l.e., 2nd house. The closer the Yoga (lthasala, etc.), the
more certain will be the result. If in a question bearing on
marriage, the aspect between the rulers of ascendant and the
7th is a square, the event will no doubt happen but cer ~in
obstacles will have to be overcome. The Moon's situation
always gives a clue to the mind of the querent or his agent.
ln order to determine a correct answer, the different shades
of evidence have to be carefully weighed and assessed. The
astrologer must get into the root of the question and boldly
declare the answer avoiding subjective factors. Ali questions
should always be related to their appropriate housès and the
planets located therain or connected with the houses 6y
ownership, etc., should be earefully considered:
Translation, of light is another factor considered in this
book. WhEm a planet separates from one that is slower than
itself and overtakes another by conjunction or aspect, there
is translation of light. The object of a querv will be realised
through the help of a person sjgnified by the translating
planet. Thus if in a question beaririq on marriage, Mercury
translates the light of' the- lord of the 7th to the lord of the
xiv
1 st, the result will turn out favourably through the person
indicated by the translating planet if the aspect is good. If
the aspect is evil, th en there will be failure or disappointment through the person signified by the translating planet.
As in horoscopy, very often the rime-factor is a hard nut
to crack in horary astrology too. The most important part of
a question is when the expected result or event will occur.
There are no doubt rules given Jn texts on horary astrology .
but most of them are not found to be quite reliable. It occurs
·to me that every case must be decided on the nature of the
question concerned. Suppose a question relatinq to birth of
an issue is put when the woman is, in an advanced stage of
preqnancv and at the time of the query, the lords of' the
ascendant and the significator are 2° apart from each other.
Then we can safely say that the delivery will take place
within two weeks and the probable date can be fixed on the
basis of transits. If a similar distance (conjunction or
favourable aspect) is found between the significators, say in
respect of a question on marriage, decide the time on the
basis of the movable, fixed or common nature of the sign the
significator is in. According to the fa mous work Krishneeya,
the number of constellations intervening between the ascendant and the constellation occupied by the significator indicates the number of days, provided the ascendant is in a
fixed sign. This is to be doubled or trebled according as the
Lagna is a common or movable sign respectively.
Another method given by the same work is as follows :
Multiply the number of signs intervening between the ascendant and the/siqn held by the significator by 12. The product
representa the number of days, months or years according as
the ascendant is a .movable, a common or a fixed siqn. Both
xv
1
the ·above two methods have not been found quite satisfactory. , A more satisfactorv method is toconsider the number of
degrees between the aspect of the ascendarit lord and the
significator as representi,ng the number of days, weeks, or
months according as the ascendant is movable, common or
1 fixed.
The other method which could be used with considerable
success is to consider the question chart, as Varshaphal
chart, calcu late the Varshaphal -Dasas and then time the
result. This method has a drawback because, the timing is to
be confined to within 12 months. The reader will do weil to
adopt the method appealing to him in the light of the practical exarnples given in this book. It is generally difficult to
judge the time of occurrence with accuracy. But by practice
a student of astrology can instinctively feel which method he
shoùld adopt and he can generally be successfu 1. ,
Horary 'astrology is a system of prognostication which
demonstrates that in the time at which a query is put 1 ies
imbedded seeds of the result. The astrologer in his interpretation of the horarv chart should be assisted by prompting~
from the great Divine.
The astrologer who practises this branch.of astrology has
certain moral responsibilities. If for instance a query is put
about the death of an ail it'lg father, the answer should not be
readilv given. The astrologer should tirst of ail find. out
from the chart, the motive .of the querent -whether the
question is prompted by love and affection or by sinister
motives. such as grabbîng the father's property. Simi larly
questions- -bearing on the ,character of the wife or husband
should not be answered if' the motives of the querents are
xvi
suspect. Thus the astrological student has certain moral
responsibilities towards the society and his art should not ba
used for harrning others or for creatinq fear or panic or
suffering.
A novice in Horary astrology must be very careful in
interpreting the Prasna chart.
With my humble capacity 1 have tried my level best ta
present an English translation of Prasna Tantra as lucidly as
possible and it is for the educated public to judge how far 1
have been successful in this endeavour. While placing this
translation in the hands of my readers 1 crave their indulgence for any fault that may have crept in.
If the book should prove instructive and 1 usetul to my
readers, 1 shall' teel myself rewarded.
BANGALORE,
8-8-1969
S.V. RAMAN

Stanzas 1 and 2 :---According to the opinion of
astrologers, ail those human beings who will be
impelled (by Daiva) wish to know the current good
and bad nature of things in their lives.
Once upon a time, even the lord of the world
Brahma approached Vishnu to learn horary
astrology.
NOTES
The importance of horary astroloqv ' is stressed in
the first two stanzas. Every one that is impelled by Oaiva
or an inner urge will become inclined to appro ach an astreloger ta know about his immediate future. Here 'Dai va , is
the unknown factor and usually means ona's past Karma. As
2 Prasna Tantra
a result of one's past Karma, one gets prerana or inspiration
or the urge to consult an astroloper and horary astrology
. reveals the future on the basis of the current dispositions of
planets.
How to Put A Query?

Stanza 3 : -One should approach a learned
astrologer of pleasant disposition, early in the
morning, with fruits, flowers and money and facing
the east ana after making due abeieance ta the
astrologer, put only one question of an auspicious
nature.
NOTES
According to tradition the querist, be he a commoner or a
prince, should follow a certain procedure before putting
questions to an astroloqer. Early morning is considered to be
suitable for -consultation .. Howers, fruits, etc., indicate auspiciousness. An astroroqer is a daivagnya or decipherer of the
intention of God. Therefore he is to be approached with a
certain reverence and humility. It is only an astrologer of a
"pleasant disposition" that deserves to be consulted. The
above three stanzas are attributed to Varahamihira.

Stanzas 5 and 6 :- The first query is to be read
from the ascendant, the second from the Moon ,
the third from the Sun, the fourth from Jupiter
and the fifth from the stronger of' the pla nets
Mercury or Venus.
It is the diètum of the astrological science that
a query has to be answered by a proper assessrnent
of the ascendant. If only one question is answered,
the prediction cannet go wrong.
NOTES
The consldered opinion of the author appears to be that
at a time only one question has to be tackied if the prediction
6 Prasna Tantra
is to be correct. When a series of questions are put at a
particular moment, they have to be answered thus :
1 st question-Ascendant;
2nd question-Sign occupied by the Moon;
3rd question-Sign occupied by the Sun;
. 4th question- Sign occupied by Jupiter;
5th question Sign occupied by Mercury or Venus.
ln regard to the 5th question, the Rasi held by either Mercury
or Venus whichever is stronger should be considered. .
ln support of his view that only one query should be
answered from a horary chart. Nilakanta quotes from Presne
Chinthamani according to which after making ail the calculations only one query should be answered.

Stanza 1 :-Whether according to horoscopy or
h~rary astrology, whichever, Bhavas are associated
with or aspected bv either their respective lords or
benefic planets would gain vitality. The effects will
be destroyed if malefics aspect or join them.
NOTES
This is a general principle enumerated in ail astrological
~orks. Stanza 1 makes clear the following three combinatiens: •
(i) The Bhava aspected by or associated with its lord will
prosper,
(ii) The Bhava aspected by or associated with a benefic
planet will also prosper.
(iii) The Bhava aspected by or conjoined with a malefic
will Jose vitality.

Stanzas 10 to 12 :-A query such as "when will
1 succeed" should be answered thus: There will be
suc cess when (a) the lord of the ascendant aspects
the signification and the significator aspects the
20 Prasna Tantra
ascendant; or (b) the significator being situated in
the ascendant aspects the lord of the ascendant; (c)
or the significator occupying a sign other than the
ascendant aspects the lord 'of the ascendant; 01 (d)
the significator aspects the Lagna, Lagna lord or the
Moon : (e) or the ascendant lord and the significator
are mutually connected either in the ascendant or
in the Bhava (house) concerned. ln (b) and (c) the
success will be immediate or delayed respectively.
NOTES
Stanzas 10 to 12 are important. They enable one ta answer, whether the object of the query will be realised immediately or after sorne delay. The terms signification and
significator used by us in the translation mean the Bhave or
house in question and its lord respectively. Thus in a query
"when will one succeed in getting married ". the signification
has réference ta the 7th house and the significator, ta the
lord of the 7th. There will be marriage very saon if (a) the
7th lord occupying the ascendant aspects the lord of the
rising sign, (b) or the marriage takes place after delay if thé7th lord not occupying the Lagna aspects the ascendant lord.

Stanzas 14 to 16 underline the tact that any connection
between the lord of a house and the lord of the 6th or the 8th
will adversely affect the Bhava concerned. Thus if the 4th
Bhava lord is connected with the lord of the 6th or the Bth,
the mother will suffer ; if it is the 5th Bhava lord, the children
Bhava Prasna 31
will suffer and so on. Here the point to be noted is that
lords of the 6th and the 8th are first-rate malefics.
Ali questions pertaining to brothers, sisters, shortjourneys
and neighbours should bè ascertained from the third house.
If the third is occupied by malefics, especially Mars or
Rahu, there will be discord between the querent and his
brothers. If the affliction is severe, the brothers will be
bitter enemies.
Questions, pertaining to the disappearance of brothers
and their whereabouts, should also be answered on the basis
of the third house. If, for instance, a querv pertains to a
brother who has left the horne and the lord of the 3rd is in
8th afflicted, the brothers life may be in danger. This is
one view. The other reliable view is, treat the 3rd house as
the Lagna of the brother and study the events as reckoned
trom the 3rd. Thus if a query pertains to the profession of
the brothers, ascertain how the lord of the 3rd and the lord
of the 10th therefrom (i.e., 12th from the ascendant) are
mutually disposed. An Ithasala between these two lords
indicate~ that the brother's professional prospects are indeed
bright. This method gives more accu rate results. From the
3rd house can also be answered such events as to whether
rumeurs. etc., are true or false and whether messages sent
by post or otherwise will evoke response and if so the
nature of su ch replies, etc. The first house always stands
for the querant. Select the house which stands for the
relative concerned (e.g., 5th for son, 9th for Iather , etc.)
and see if there is Ithasala between the two lords. If the
lords happen to be malefics, and in hostile aspect. a hostile
reply or message will be received. If they are benefics but
in hostile aspect, favourable response ls possible after
32 . Prasna Tantra
sorne delay. If they are benefics and the aspect ls also
beneficial, a favourable message can be expected without
delay.

40 Prasna Tantra
~o foolproof method has so ffar been devised thouqh sorne
astrologers have been able to forecast the date on the basis
of the conception tirne, as per the methods given \ in Briti«: • \ 1
Jataka and other standard works.
Suppose at the time of question, 25° 30' of Leo rises,
this means the 8th Navamsa is rising and in the 8th 2° 10'
nas passed. (7 Navamsas x 3° 20' = 23° 20': 25° 30' -
23° 20' = 2° 10'.)
According to Tajaka, the woman is in her 8th month of
pregnancy and her delivery is likely in about 2 months' time.
As each Navarnsa (3° 20')' is equ ival ent to 30 days, 2° 20'
( ... h 8 h N . 30 rernarmnq ln t e t avamsa) will be equal to (-- x 2°20')
.3°20'
= 12 days, which means she will deliver after 1 rnonth and
12 days trom the date of question. 1 have dealt in greater
details with the question of timing events in horary astrology
in the Introduction to this book. But the method given is
this stanza does not seem to work in actual practice: It can,
not worked at least in the majority of cases.

According to stanzas 45 to 48, the ascendant, the 7th, the
10th and the 4th respectively signify the physician (or
surgeon), the disease '(or illness), the patient and the treatment.
Malefics in the ascendant are no indication of the illness
being cured. If maiefics are in the 10th, the treatment will
do no good due to the patient's own fault such as incorrect
dieting, etc. If the affliction is centered on the 4th and 7th,
then complications set in. Benefics will enable the patient to
get relief. But if they are retrograde, after some siqns of
recovery, the patient will have a relapse. And if the malefies are in Ithasala with the Moon, death will result.
Disease is assigned to 7th house. But in our humble
opinion, the 6th house should be considered as signljfying
disease and the judgment should be made on the basis cf the
ascendant, its lord, planets in it. the 6th house, the laird of
the 6th and the planets in the 6th and the Yogas caused
between thèse two sets of factors. If the concerned factors,

sign, the patient becomes relieved saon; if thesiqn is fixed,

be neither long nor short.

the 6th in the ascendant, the patient has ta suffer a lot and
for a long time too. If the 6th and 8th lords are both in the
ascendant, death ensues. If the lords of the 6th and 8th
interchanqe, there will be speedy recovery. These observations are due to our own studies and, therefore, the readers

Stanzas 70 and 71 :-If the query "whether
my angry wife will return home" is put, say that
she will not return if the Sun is in the 15t, 2nd or 3rd
house. and Venus is in the 5th, 6th or 7th. If

NOTES
The couple quarrel and the wife leaves the husband. He
wants to know whether or not she would return back ta hirn.

58 Prasna Tantra
4th and Venus is in the houses 5, 6 and 7; (b) she will return

she will return early if the Moon is waxing.
The following observations based on my experience are
for the guidance of the readers :
Whether it is the wife or the husband that puts the
question, the 1 st hou se signifies the querent and the 7th the
partner. We can safely predict that a married couple living
separately at the time of query will live again together if the
lord of the ascendant is in the 7th unafflicted or the lords of
the ascendant and the 7th are in mutual favourable aspect.
The retrograde positions of the two lords do not favour
reconciliation. On the other hand, if one of the lords is
retrograde, the person signified by the lord (e.g. 7th-wife)
will be recalcitrant but they will. come together if the aspect
is favourable.
Ouesrions bearing on divorce can also be answered under
the above combinations. Lords of the 1 sv and 7th in mutual
evil aspect and afflicfed by Rahu or Mars is an indication of
impending divorce. 1 f the two lords are in mutual evil aspect
but conjoined by Jupiter or Venus, the desire for divorce
will give place to the desire for reconciliation. If the Lagna
lord is atfticred by Mars or Rahu, the querant is desrr ous of

According to stanza 109, one can gain office or preferment in his job under the following conditions:
(a) Lord of the ascendant should be in the 1 Oth and vice
versa, both unafflicted. '
78 Prasna Tantra
(b) Lord of the 10th in Muthasila with a benefic planet.
The result will be unfavourable if in the combination
(b), of the planets in Muthasila, the one with slower velocity
is afflicted either by conjunction with or aspect of malefics.
Suppose .Iord of the 10th is Mars and he is in Muthasila
with Mercury. Mars is the slower-moving planet. If Mars is
in conjunction with the Sun, Saturn or Rahu, then the
benefic yoga is neutralised and the querent will get no
preferment, promotion or acquisition.

Sranzas 110 and 111 :-If a slow-moving planet
is strongly placed in the 4th in adverse Muthasila
(square aspect) the querent's reputation is in
jeopardy; if in favourable Muthasila (trine) free
trom affliction, he gets fame. If the Moon is weak
and the slow-moving planet is not devoid of
strength, the querent gets the kingdom after visiting
many places. If the ascendant lord is in the ascendant
and Mars is exalted, the estate is secured.
NOTES
There is also another interpretàtion in a manuscript secured
by me from a Pandit, viz., if the 4th house is aspected by
malefics, it affects the reputation ; if by benefics, the querent
gets fame. But this interpretation is not endorsed by Tajaka
commenta tors.

the lord Venus is in 18° Libra, The lord of the 7th (minister)
is in the 7th, Aries 22° and the Moon is in Aquarius 210.

seventh. Karnboola is present as the-Moon is having Ithasala
with both the lords. Hence the answer should be friendship
exists between the ruler and his minister.

Stanzas 120 and 121 :-The query "whether or
not the king holds the country permanently" should
be answered thus: If out of the lords of the ascendant and the 10th, the slower-moving lord is in a
quadrant, the authority will be permanent. Otherwise,
the sway will be temporary.
If the slower-moving planet is retrograde and
occupies the 4th house and there is Kamboola
Yoga, the kingdom will slip out of the king's rule
for sorne time but he gets it back quickly. If there
is Musaripha, the chance of getting back the kingdom is bleak.
NOTES
Stanza 120 requires that out of the lords of the 1 st and
10th, the slower-moving should be in a quadrant ta make the
ruler's hold permanent. Accord ing to stanza 121, the kingdom will slip out of his hands and it will be restored to him '
again if the slower lord, referred to in stanza 120, is in the
4th, retrograde, and there is Kamboola Yoga. If there is
Musaripha. the loss is permanent.

88 Prasna Tantra
According ta some commentators, the movable, fixed or
common nature of the sign refers ta the position of the lord
of the 11 th and not the Moon.
According ta this interpretation, the lord of the 11 th is
in a common or dual sign in the illustration. The result is
uncertainty. We teel the latter interpretation is more reasonable. We would like ta split up the combination th us :
(a) There should be Kamboola between the Moon and
the 11 th lord.
(b) The 4th lord should be in a Kendra.
(c) The nature of this sign should b~ considered.
The following illustration will make the above three
points clear.
Lagna-6 Moon-5
Sun 9
.lup-12
Venus 14
(a) The Moon has Kamboola with Jupiter lord of the 11 th.
(b) The Sun ';s in a quadrant
(c) Jupiter occupies a fixed sign. Hence the results are
indicated by the nature of this sign.
Bhava Prasna 89
It will be seen that Kamboola can occur only when there
is Ithasala between the lord of Lagna and the significator, the
Moon himself having Ithasala with either of these two lords.

Stanza 129 :-If the query is about war with
an enemy, say the enemy is powerful if the lord of
-the 7th is strongly disposed. If the / lord of the
12th is powertul, then the person for whom the
query is put is more strongly placed.
NOTES
ln horary astrology. the 6th should be taken 'to represent
secret foes and the 7th should be taken to signify open
enemies, such as contestants in elections, plaintiff or defendant in a civil case, the prosecutor in a ~riminal case, etc.
The ascendant always signifies the querent. If the lord of the
7th is afflicted. it will have adverse effects on the opponent.
If the lord of the 6th is afflicted, the secret foe (especially
relatives) will suffer.' If the lord of the ascendant .is weil
placed and strong, the querent will succead.

Stanza 130 :-If the 12th house is weil aspected
and conjoined, the querent will spend money on
deserving causes. Otherwise the earning will be
squandered. ln this way, each house should be
studied.
NOTES
ln the final analysis, whichever 8hava and its lord are
strengthened, weil aspe cted or conjoined. good results of the
8hava manifest. If the 8hava and the lord are afflicted, the
events signified by the 8hava suffer.

Stanza 1 ~-If the lord of Ithe Lagna is in
Muthasila with a planet in the tagna or the 10th
house, the traveller returns safely. If the lord of
the ascendant is in the 7th or 4th or is in Muthasila
with the lord of the 7th, he will retum with' much
difficulty.
NOTES
The Lagna signifies the traveller or the missing person.
The 7th rules the 'way', the 4th signifies the traveller's happiness. An lthasala of the lord of the ascendant with a planet
in the ascendant or the 10th favours safe return. The ascendant lord in the 4th or 7th or in Ithasala with the lord of the
7th signifies return with difficulty.
We would like to substitute the significator's house (e.g.,
5th for son, 7th for wife or husband, etc.) for the traveller
or absent persan. thé 7th therefrorn for the way and the 4th
therefrom for traveller's happiness.

his own accord if the lord of the 7th is in the ascendant.

Stanza 22 :- The "fugitive will return from the
foreign country in as many months as the number of
signs intervening between the lagna and the stronqest
planet.
NOTES
Suppose in a Prasna chart which indicates the return ota
traveller the Lagna ls Sagittarius and the strongest planet
. happens to be the Moon situated in Cancer. The intervening
number of signs from the ascendant to Cancer is seven.
Therefor~ the fug itive may return in seven months' time.

Stanza 23 : -The above period of return ;5 for
movable signs. According as the strongest planet is
in a fixed or a common sign, the number is to be
doubled or trebled.
NOTES
Suppose the number of signs intervening between the
ascendant and the strongest planet is 6 and the planet is in
a fixed sign. Then the time of return will be 12 rnonths. 1 f
the planet considered is in a. common sign, the period of
return will be 18 months.

Stanza 27 :-The traveller will meet with as many
benefits on the way as the number of benefic planets
tnat are occupvinq the 9th or 12th trom the Lagna or
Lagna lord.
NOTES
This combination is 50 worded that it is not possible to
apply it unless inferential ability helps the astrologer.
Supposinq the 9th place from the ascendant lord has three
malefics or three benefics. Then the traveller has to encounter three difficulties or three benefits on the way. What
is the nature of the difficulty or the .benefit and how it is to
be inferred in the light of present-day conditions are matters
which the astrologer has to decide by himself.

Stanza 28 :-According as afthcred Saturn devoid
of beneficial aspects is in the 9th or 8th, the traveller
suffers from illness, or dies in a foreign place.
NOTES
Here affliction means association with malefics or being
aspected by them. If Saturn is with Mars or the Sun and not
having the aspect or association of Venus or Jupiter, he is
considerablyafflicted. Wh en he is in the 9th, the traveller
suffers sickness. When he is in·the 8th, the traveller will die

Stanzas 36 to 42 have been reproduced by Neelakantha from
Prithuyasu's Shetpenchesikhe, a rare work on Horary Astrelogy. They are intended to predict victory on war, . defeat of
the enemy or his retreat, etc. According to stanza 36, the
enemy will run awav atter beingdefeated in the battle-field
if malefics are in the 4th The first line of stanza given in
~ ~

Tantra. However, Neelakantha's version is to be preferred.
Stanza 37 says that if the 4th bouse falls in Pisces. Scorpio
or Aquarius or Cancer, the enemy is vanquished. He is beaten
and then he takes to heels if a quadrupedal sign is in the 4th.
Quadrupedal signs are Taurus, Aries, Leo and Saqittarius. The
words Chatushpadaihi palayanam is translated by some
authors as "retreat with quadrupeds ", Neelakantha's cornmentator clearly says satroh palayanam bhavet, i.e., the enemy

retreats if the 4th house is a quadrupedal sign - chatursthane
chatushpada ras; gataihi.
Even without being occupied by planets, defeat stares the
enemy if in the Pras na chart Lagna or the 4th house falls in

previous one, except that the word "Laqna " is added.
Benefics in the 4th are not recommended as it indicates
enemy's aggression. On the contrary, the Sun or the Moon in
the 4th faveurs the victim of aggression (Stanza 39).
Stanza 40 warns us that no prediction, good or bad,
should be given in case a fixed sign rises occupied by Jupiter
or Saturn. But when malefics are in the 3rd, 5th and 6th, conflict with the enemy is shown. ln the first part of the stanza.
the reference is to the situation of Jupiter and Saturn. The
word veekshita (aspect) is used in another version of Shetpanchasikha.
Stanza 41 isclear and needs no explanation.
Regarding the time factor as to when a threatened aggression can happen, stanza 42 indicates a principle which, it
occurs to us, does not work, at least in modern times. If
suppose the ascendant at the time of the query is Aries and
the Moon is in Scorpio , then the number of signs intervening
in between these two factors is 8 and the invasion may take
place within 8 days. If there is a planet in between the ascen~
dant and the Moon, then this princip le cannet be applied.
Many of these combinations were framed at a time when
mutual aggressions between petty chiefs were the order of
~he day. But we can adapt them to suit modern conditions
oniy after considerable research

120 Prasna Tantra
NOTES'
Stanzas 64 to 66 give combinations for knowing the cause
of disease brought about by factors other than organic. The
belief is still widely held that the spirits of dead persons and
female and male deities are responsible for bringing about
diseases which are of a psvchosomatic nature. 1 do not propose
to en large upon the rationale of belief in such things.
Researches in physical phenomena made in Europe and
America have beenlendinq strong confirmation to Ithe theory
that the human personality survives physical death and that
depending upon the nature and inclination of ones existence
in the physical life, the persona lit y after death assumes different types of existence favourinq or persecuting their near
and dear ones according to the harmonious and hostile
relationships they had with the aftected persons, while living
on the earth.
A arete is a disembodied soul soon after death. It is said
that the curse of a prete towards a higher plane of existence
depends on the satisfactory performance of the obsequies by
the sons or dependents of the deceased concerned. When
these ceremonies are not properly performed the preta is
denied safe conduct with the result curses are sent to the
persons responsible for such omission.
Bhutas are different spirits or disembodied soul frustrated
in their earth-life.
Ambadevis are female deities resident ln watery places.
Devi mentioned in stanza 64 is interpreted by the cornmentator as a female preta or disembodied spirit. Swagotradevi is the family deitv.
Sakini is a female divinity of an inferior character
attendant specially on Siva and Ourga.
On Special Questions 121
If the planet occupying the 8th or ·12th is Rahu, the
disease is due to pretas ; Jupiter-parents; the Moon-Ambudevi; the Sun-Devi; Saturn-family deity; Mercury-Bhutas;
Mars - Sakini ; and Venus-Ambudevi.
The subject of "deities " causing diseases is fâscinating
and 1 have dealt with it exhaustively in my English translation
of Prasna Marga. '

(!';;~ '<f ~~~~ iT;{t itiTlf~~à Il' 19 Il

Stanza 89:--When the Moon is not in a Kendra
the direction is signified by his position from the
nearest quadrant, the reckoni.ng commencing from the
ascendant as east and every 45° there from representing a cardinal point in· the order of east, south-east,
south-west. north, north-west and north-east; or the
direction is.signified by the nature of the rising sign.
NOTES
Suppose the ascendant is 12° Aries and the Moon is, say
in 8° Virgo. Here the nearest Kendra or quadrant is the 7th,
1 the Moon being 34° behind. The ascendant (Aries 12°) 1
represents the east, the 7~h (Libra 12°) represents west. The
4th (Cancer 12°) rules the north and the 10th (Capricorn 12°)
represents the south.
The Moon is between the IV (north) and VII (west)
but more than 45° from the 4th. Hence the direction is west.
If supposing the Moon were exactly 45° from the 4th, \ then
the direction would have been north-west.
Suppose the ascendant is 20° C_ancer and the Moon is in
Virgo 5°. The ascendant represents the east and the 4th
represents the north. 45° indicates north-east. If supposing
132 Prasna Tantra
the Moon wère in 10° Virgo which mearis rrearer 'thé 4th
house and hence the direction' will be north.
Another method is also qiven, If the rising siqn-is .fi~ry.
the direction is south-east; if earthy, south-west; if airy,
north-west and if watery, north east, To put it more clearly,
this formula is also to be applied when the Moon is not in a
Kendra,

Stanza 112 :-If "has he committed any theft

NOTES

. trom the charge of thieving. The Moon's Muthasila with a
malefic confirrns that the suspected person is the real thief.
The suspect can howe'ver be considered to have committed a
theft on a previous occasion if the ascendant lord or the
Moon is in Muthasila with the lord of the 7th.

As long as a strong Venus is in Lagna, the authority
will be able to hold the person in custody. If Saturn
or Venus is in combustion, the chances for slippinq

birth chart, if present in Prasna chart, indicates

Stanza 185 :-'11 the question is "shall 1 sel! the

NOTES
Stanzas 183 to 185 will be found to be very useful in the
daily,lives of businessmen, merchants and even corn mon
people for buying and selling articles.
170 Prasna Tantra
The questions 'are: (a) Can 1 purchase this article 7 (b)
Can 1 sell this article?
The idea of purchasing an article is to sell it for a profit
and the object of selling an article is to get profit. The Lagna
lord presents the purchase, while the lord of the 2nd or the
11 th is the significator of the sale. It wi Il be to the advantage
of the querent to purchase the article if the ascendant and
its lord are strongly disposed.
The querent will get good profit wh en the 11 th house is
strongly disposed, i.e., occupied by powerful planets.
The word "Agrapathi" is translated by comrnentators as
lord of the 10th while some say it means either the lord of
the 2nd or the 11 th. If the former meaning is accepted, then
as per stanza 183, the lord of the 10th becomes the indicator
of contempJated sale. '
We have dealt with hundreds of questions under this category. When the lords of the ascendant and the 11 th are in
mutual trines or sextiles. one can easily purchase the article
in view. The combination has to be slightly varied byour
intelligence according to the nature of the 'article' involved.
If it is house or landed propertv, Mars should not be afflicted.
If it is vehicle, Venus should not be afflicted. If it is a
business èoncern, Mercury should not be afflicted. If it is a
factory, Saturn should not be afflicted. An astrologer can
clearly advise his client on the basis of the three stanzas
given above subject to his own intelligent interpretation.

the significator is the lord of the t Oth. And the house referred
to in the above two stanzas is the appropria te Bhava.

Stanzas 13 and 14 :-If the Lagna is not aspected
by a benefic, the benefic effect is one-fourth. When
a benefic aspects the lord of the ascendant, the
benetic effect is one-half. When a benefic aspects the
ascendant, the good effect is three-foUrths which
means the object will be fulfilled.
NOTES
ln the matter of judging an event, the strong disposition
of the ascendant and its lord are essential. When a benefic
planet aspects the ascendant. the benefic outcome will be
75% and consequently we may declare success- When on Iy
the lord is aspected by a benefic, the good affect is only 50%'
which means the chances of success are even. When there is
no aspect on the Lagna. the result is not favourable.

Stanzas 15 to 19 :-Reduce the longitude of the
ascendant (beginning trom Aries) into minutes. -lt is
called Kalapinda. Multiply this by the equinoctial
shadow at the moment of query. Divide this product by 7. Reject the quotient. The remainder
represents the planets (in the order the Sun--1, the
Moon-2, Mars-3, Mercury-4, Jupiter-5,
Venus=-û and Saturn-7) which is to be taken.
Multiply again the Kalapinda by Hw concerned
planetary factor : (the Sun-5, the Moon--21 , , Mars-14, Mercury-9, Jupiter-8, Venus and
Saturn -11), Oivide the product by the sum of the
Gunakas from the Sun to the planet in question.
Deduct from this remainder (y) the Gunaka of the
Sun, etc, Whichever planets Gunaka is not deductable should be considered the rising planet, If this
planet is a benefic, the object of the query will be
fulfilled. The time of fructification is indicated by
the remainder (y). If the risinq . planet is the Sun or
Mars, the remainder (y) répresents so many days.

fortnight. If it is Jupiter, the number represents month,
if Mercury-halt-year and if Saturn, years.

Stanzas 21 [0 24 :-The Sun, Mars, Venus,
Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn and the Moon represent
respectively the groups of letters commencing from
a, ka. cha, ta, tha, pa, ya and sa. On the basis of
the first letter or syllable uttered by a querent the
Lagna at night should be found and good and bad
predicted. Ifthefirstletter isthe 1st, 3rd, etc.,.in
181
Miscellaneous Matters
the group, then the Lagna is an odd sign. If it is 2nd
or 4th in the group, it is an even sign. .
As the Sun and the Moon have only one Hasl
each, the question of an odd or even Rasi does not
arise. Hence one should predict as before on the
basis of the ascendant. This ls the method used for
deciding Lagnas when more than one query is put.

Suppose the letter uttered by the querent is Ma, Then the
ruler is Saturn and the rising sign should be taken as Capricorn or Aquarius. Sirice Ma is an odd letter, the ascendant is
Aquarius.
When the first letter uttered by a querent cames under a
benefic planet, good can be predicted. If it co mes under a
malefic planet, unfavourable results should be anticipated.
By deciding the Lagna on the basis of the first letter and
placing the planets as on that day, the Prasna chart can be
answered as an Aroodha.

the 5th or the 9th. then the query refers to Dhatu.

another planet, the query relates to Jeeva. If the

and aspects another Navamsa in the. Lagna, the
query relates to .Jeeva.

suqqestive and concise.

Stanzas 32 and 33 :- The question will be
about a person who is closely connected with . the
188 Prasna Tantra
querentIt a planet is strongly placed in the ascendant; if in the 3rd, the query will be about
brothers; if in the 5th, about children; if in the
4th, about mother or wife; if in the 6th, about the
enemy ; if in the 7th, about wife ; if in the 9th, about
\fighteous people; if in the 10th, about preceptor.

inimical to the lord of the ascendant.
NOTES
'If the query "who is the person 1 am thinking about" is
put, then it can be .answered on the basis of stanzas 32 and
33. The house occupied bya planet endowed with strength
gives the clue to the person the querent has in mind; and if
such a house is the 3rd, it is about brothers; if it is the 6th,
it is about enemies, etc. When more than one planet occupies
a house or when more than one house is occupied by
planets, the astrologer should carefully discern the strongest
house and then give his finding.
If the lord of Navamsa Lagna occupies the Lagna, then
the query will be about the querent himself. If the lord of the
Navamsa Lagna is a friend of the lord of Lagna, the query is
about a friend. If the Navamsa Lagna lord is an enemy of
the Lagna lord, then the query is about the querent's enemy.

Stanzas 50 and 51 :- Of the 3rd and 11 th
aspect, the aspect on the 3rd is 40 % strong ; on the
11 th it is 10% ; the 3rd and 11 th aspect is secretly
friendly _ and is always favourable for fulfilment of
desires. The 4th and 10th aspect is afso 751% strong.
It ts an unfavourable and unfriendly aspect. The
Ist and 4th aspect is 100% strong. lt is secretly
hostile and unfavourable.
NOTES
Stanzas 49 to 51 deal with aspects according to Tajaka
theory. The following aspects are considered :
Trine :-When two pla nets are 120° apart, i.e., in the 5th
and the 9th from each other. it is 75% full, the strongest and
the most benefic of ail aspects and den otes fulfilment.
Sextile :-When two pla nets are in the 3rd and the 11 th
from each other (60° apart) this aspect is caused. The aspect
, on the 3rd is 40% strong, and the aspect on the 11 th is 10%
strong. This is a' beneficial aspect giving rise to the sa me
result as the trine.
Square aspect :-When two planets are in the 4th and
the 10th tram each other (90° apart) a square aspect is
formed. l.t is 45% strong. It is an inimical aspect always
indicating difficulties, obstruction, etc.
Opposition :-When two planets are in the 7th from each
other (180°), this aspect arises. This is a 100% strong aspect
and always gives rise to unfavourable and adverse results.
Miscellaneous Mattèrs 197
ln delineatinq the results of the square and opposition
as always adverse, the inherent nature of the planets seems
ta have been ignored by the Tajaka writers. It ooeurs to me
that the nature of the planet can influence to sorne extent the
nature of the aspect. For ,instance, a square or an opposition
between Jupiter and Venus cannat be as adverse as that
between Mars and Saturn. However, in interpreting Prasna'
charts, this aspect is not taken into account for reasons best
known to Tajaka writers.

Stanzas 54 and 55 :-If a faster planet with less
longitude is backwards of' a slower planet with a
greater longitude, Ithasala Yoga is caused. If the
. latter planet is ahead of the former by a minute or
30", it is a complete lthasala. This Yoga becomes

NOTES
These Tajaka Yogas have been elaborated in the latest
edition of my book Varshaphal or The Hindu Progressed
Horoscope and the esteemed reader w.ill do weil to study this
book. 1 shall however give a brief explanation in these pages.
Saturn is the slowest moving planet and the Moon the
fastest. The speed becomes faster in the order of Saturn,
Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury and the Moon.
Take the chart given below. According to stanza 54,
the Sun a faster-moving planet having Jesser longitude is
backwards of Mars the slower-rnovinq planet in greater

longitude. For an exact square aspect to occur, the
difference is 14° and this falls within the Deepthamsa (orb of
the aspect). Hence 'Ithasala Yoga is present. ln ether words,
it is an applying aspect here .
When the aspecting bodies are within a degree of the
exact aspect or in exact aspect, It is a Poorna or complete
Yoga. Ithasala Yoga is also known as Muthasila.
Easarapha Yoga
~ ~) W'~,~ ~t~ ~~ff 1

Stanza 56 :-When a faster-moving planet is
ahead of a slower-moving planet bv one degree,
Easarapha is caused. It is also called Musaripha.
This is an unfavourable combination.
NOTES
The faster-moving planet (e.g., Mercury) should be ahead
of the slower-moving planet (e.g., Venus) by 10, i.e., it is a
separating aspect and connotes that results will be unfavourable. If suppose the faster-moving planet (e.g., Mercury) is
backwards.of the slower-moving planet (e.g., Venus) byone
degree, the process in stanza 56 is reversed. Then vou have
an applying aspect and it is Ithasala. ln other words, Musaripha is also Ithasala when the aspect is separating.

Kamboola Yoga
a-~tt.l'm<i'i~i\ lrià: :q;:l{)s~ E~~f.t'cll?ft ,

Stara 61 :-When there is Ithasala between
two plan, ets and the' Moon is also involved in
Ithasala' with one of the planets, the resulting Yoga
is Kamboola. There are three varieties of this yoga,
viz., exalted, mediocre and debilitated.
Thus ends Neelakanta's Presne Tantra
NOTES
When two planets are in Ithasala and the Moon is in
Ithasala with either of them, Kamboola Yoga is caused. It
is par excellence, medium or ordinary. according to the
strenqth the Moon and the other two planets become
endowed with.
ln the exarnple chart, Jupiter and Venus are in lthasala.
The'Moon i's also in Ithasala with both. Therefore Kamboola
Yoga is caused. Jupiter and Venus are bath exalted. And
hence it is a par excellence'Kamboola.
ln Kamboola Yoga three planets are involved, viz., the
lord of iagna, the lord of event (siqniticator) and the Moon.
Miscellaneous Matters 203
Venus 12
;
,
Jupt. 14
Moon 10
When the two lords and the Moon are exalted or in their own
houses, it is Uttamottama. When the Moon is exalted and
the other two planets are in their own Navamsa, Drekkana,
Hadda, etc., it is Madhymottama. When the Moon is not
exalted, but the other lords are in own Navamsas, Drekkanas,
etc. it is Utteme. When the Moon is exalted or occupies
ow~ house and the other two lords are debilitated,.etc., it is
Uttamadhama. When the Moon is in its: own Navamsa and
Owadasamsa and the other two planets are in their own or
exalted places, it is Madhyamottama. It is Msdhyama if the
Moon is in his own Navamsa or Orekkana and is in lthasala
with the lord of Lagna in own house or Hadda. It will be
Adhama if no ne of the three planets have panchadhikara
(See Chapter III of Varshaphal). Thus the benefic, more
benefic or most benefic nature of the Kamboola Yoga should
be ascertained on the basis of the inherent strenqth of the
Moon and other planets.
With the desc;ription of sorne of these important Yogas,
Neelakanta's Prasna Tantra ends.

SOME EXAMPLES
1 am glvmg 12 examples chosen from the innumerable
number of horary questions answered by me. They will
illustrate sorne of the important principIes.
Horary astrology comes in handy for answering any type
of questions whether or not one knows his or her birth
details. Each 'house signifies certain events but the student
of astrology a lone should be able to assigri through experience, the innurnerable events of today to appropriate hou ses.
If a question such as "Whether it would be safe to travel by
air" is put, one has to adapt the ancient tool to suit the
modern conditions of travet. If it is a long trip, say overseas,
take the 9th house. If it is a trip within the country, take the
3rd house; and if it is within ones own' state, say a local
trip, take the 12th house as the significator. Sometimes, the
7th house also gives clues for predicting a fomign trip
provided the lord of the 9th is in the 7th.
Basic formulas are provided by ancient texts which can
be considered common to ail charts but Interpretative
methods become varied in view of the changed political,
economie. transport and other conditions.
It is always best to frame the question ill a clear manner,
e.ç., "Will 1 marry?" And as a matter of corollarv, the
subsidiary question-ewhen, will automatically arise. With
slight modifications, the bouses in a horary chart signify
more or less the same events as in a natal chart and bya
Sorne ExamplE1s 205
consideration of the. relative dispositions, strength, afflictions,
l, etc.. of the significators, any type of question can be answered successfully. Here again the judgment will have to
rest on interpretative ab1jlity or intuition of the astrologer.
The reader will do J"ell, to study the following charts in
the light of the above gJneral rernarks. .
